crisis but could not avert a depression. The improvisations were
bumbling, incoherent, poorly explained; the President seemed absent, so
far as attending to the economy was concerned, during the critical
period. Even now, four and a half months after the crisis
h<span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA329.w.1.0.0.1" shape="rect"></span>it,
the government has no coherent plan of recovery. In the absence of such
a plan, it is, or soon will be, trying everything at once—flooding the
economy with money, which may not work, as I argued in chapter 5; trying
to restore output and employment by massive deficit spending, which may
not work either; bailing out the banking industry—or perhaps
confiscating much of it ("nationalization") (odd how saving the industry
and swallowing it are discussed in the same breath); reforming the
regulatory framework and as part of the reform perhaps consolidating the
myriad agencies that have a piece of the
f<span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA329.w.1.0.0.2"
shape="rect"></span>inancial regulatory pie; and relieving mortgagors of
some of the burden of their
mort</span><span id="content-0019.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA330.w.1.0.0"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0019.xml_para.353.1.0.box.155.299.901.532.q.60"
class="gtxt_body"><span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA330"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA330.w.0.0.0"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA330.w.1.0.0"
shape="rect"></span>gages. All are measures with strengths and
weaknesses that cannot be gauged in advance; all suffer from having to
be adopted without having been thought out in advance; some, such as
regulatory reform, are overly ambitious. And doubtless there is more to
come. The atmosphere is electric with proposals for economic recovery—so
many that the government may lack the intellectual resources to evaluate
them.</span>

</div>

</div>

<div style="display:none;">

<span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA330.w.1.0.0.1" shape="rect"></span>

</div>

<div style="display:none;">

<span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA330.w.1.1.0" shape="rect"></span>

</div>

<div class="flow" style="">

<span id="content-0019.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA329" class="page"
shape="rect"></span>

<div id="content-0019.xml_block.352.1.0.box.152.310.906.1613.q.60"
class="gtxt_body">

</div>

</div>

<div class="flow" style="">

<span id="content-0019.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA330" class="page"
shape="rect"></span>

<div id="content-0019.xml_block.353.1.0.box.154.299.902.1106.q.60"
class="gtxt_body">

<span id="content-0019.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA330.w.1.1.0" shape="rect"></span>

I am not a forecaster. I do not know when the recovery from this
depression will begin. But if it begins tomorrow, the trillions of
dollars that the government has spent to speed recovery, and the
restructuring of banking and its reform that will bring in their train
untold problems and uncertainties, will overhang the economy for years
to come, as when an expensive treatment cures a deadly illness but
leaves the patient debilitated.

</div>

</div>

<div class="flow" style="">

<div class="pagebreak">

</div>

<span id="content-0019.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA331" class="page"
shape="rect"></span>

<div id="content-0019.xml_block.354.0.0.box.410.559.305.129.q.90"
class="gtxt_body">

<span id="content-0019.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA331.w.0.0.0" shape="rect"></span>

<span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA331"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA331.w.0.0.0"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA332"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA332.w.0.0.0"
shape="rect"></span>Further Readings

<span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA332.w.0.0.0.1"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA332.w.0.0.0.2"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0019.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA331.w.0.1.0"
shape="rect"></span>

Index

</div>

</div>

<div class="flow" style="">

</div>

<div style="display:none;">

<span id="content-0019.xml_GBS.PA332.w.0.0.0.3" shape="rect"></span>

</div>

<span id="content-0020.xml"></span>

<div style="display:none;">

<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA333"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA333.w.0.0.0"
shape="rect"></span>

</div>

<div class="flow" style="">

<span id="content-0020.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA333" class="page"
shape="rect"></span>

<div id="content-0020.xml_block.356.0.0.box.422.560.368.48.q.80"
class="gtxt_body" style="margin-bottom: 1.5em;">

<span id="content-0020.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA333.w.0.0.0" shape="rect"></span>

Further Headings

</div>

<div id="content-0020.xml_block.356.1.0.box.152.814.906.1057.q.60"
class="gtxt_body">

<span id="content-0020.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA333.w.1.0.0" shape="rect"></span>

<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA333.w.1.0.0" shape="rect"></span>The
following is a list of books and articles that\
readers of this book may find interesting. The\
views expressed in them are diverse, and I do not\
mean by suggesting them to imply that I agree with\
all of them.\

<span id="content-0020.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA333.w.1.1.0" shape="rect"></span>

<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA333.w.1.1.0" shape="rect"></span>
Tobias Adrian and Hyun Song Shin, "Financial In-\
termediaries, Financial Stability, and Monetary\
Policy" (forthcoming in *journal of Economic*\
*Perspectives).*\
<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA333.w.1.2.0" shape="rect"></span>Dean
Baker, *Plunder and Blunder: The Rise and Fall*\
*of the Bubble Economy* (2009).\
<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA333.w.1.3.0" shape="rect"></span>Ben S.
Bernanke, "Deflation: Making Sure 'It'\
Doesn't Happen Here," Nov. 21, 2002,\
www.federalreserve.gov/BOARDDOCS/\
SPEECHES/2002/20021121/default.htm (visited\
Jan. 1, 2009).\
<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA333.w.1.4.0"
shape="rect"></span>Olivier Blanchard, "The Crisis: Basic Mechanisms,\
<span id="content-0020.xml_ORIG-GBS.PA334.w.1.0.0"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0020.xml_para.357.1.0.box.155.295.897.1501.q.50"
class="gtxt_body"><span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA334"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA334.w.0.0.0"
shape="rect"></span><span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA334.w.1.0.0"
shape="rect"></span> and Appropriate Policies" (Dec. 2008), http://\
papers.ssrn.com/sol3/papers.cfm7abstract_id =\
<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA334.w.1.1.0" shape="rect"></span>
1324280//.\
<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA334.w.1.2.0" shape="rect"></span>Markus
K. Brnnnermeier, "Deciphering the Liquid-\
ity and Credit Crunch 2007-08" (forthcoming\
in *Journal of Economic Perspectives).*\
<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA334.w.1.3.0"
shape="rect"></span>Richard C. K. Burdekin and Pierre L. Siklos, eds.,\
*Deflation: Current and Historical Perspectives*\
(2004).\
<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA334.w.1.4.0" shape="rect"></span>George
Cooper, *The Origin of Financial Crises: Cen-*\
*tral Banks, Credit Bubbles and the Efficient*\
*Market Failure* (2008).\
<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA334.w.1.5.0" shape="rect"></span>Bradv
Dennis and Robert O'Harrow Jr., "A Crack in\
the System," *Washington Post,* Dec. 29-31, 2008.\
<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA334.w.1.6.0" shape="rect"></span>Chris
Farrell, *Deflation: What Happens When Prices*\
*Fall* (2004).\
<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA334.w.1.7.0" shape="rect"></span>Niall
Ferguson, *The Ascent of Money: A Financial*\
*History of the World* (2008).\
<span id="content-0020.xml_GBS.PA334.w.1.8.0"
